# Time-series calibration results

Generated 2026-08-28 from `result_calibration_*.txt` and `logs/**/*.log` in this repo. Every row is one long-term-forecast run (`task_name=long_term_forecast`); tabular-regression calibration runs are excluded.

**4976 (config, method) results** over **2188 distinct training configs**, 10 datasets, 4 backbones.

Machine-readable companion with every field: [calibration_results_tsf.csv](calibration_results_tsf.csv). Both files are regenerated by `python scripts/collect_calibration_results.py`, which reruns daily at 06:00 via cron. Day-by-day history: [calibration_results_changelog.md](calibration_results_changelog.md).

## 0. What arrived since the last run

No change since the previous run (2026-08-27 06:00). No new logs, no new results, no value moved.

3517 of the 4976 (config, method) pairs were observed more than once (the same experiment re-run, or the same run present in both a `result_*.txt` file and a log). For **244** of them the repeats do not agree — the model was retrained, so the calibration numbers moved slightly. In that case the tables show the value from the `result_*.txt` file (the canonical record written by the calibration code) and the CSV carries `coverage_min`, `coverage_max`, `width_min`, `width_max`, `n_runs_seen` and `rerun_disagrees` so the spread stays visible.

## 2. Config encoding

Every run is identified by the `setting` string built in [run.py:281](../run.py#L281):

```
long_term_forecast_{model_id}_{model}_{dataset}_ne{num_experts}_pe{prob_expert}_ug{unc_gating}
  _ft{features}_sl{seq_len}_ll{label_len}_pl{pred_len}_dm{d_model}_nh{n_heads}_el{e_layers}
  _dl{d_layers}_df{d_ff}_expand{expand}_dc{d_conv}_fc{factor}_eb{embed}_dt{distil}
  _{des}_{itr}_seed{seed}
```

**Varying fields** (the ones in the tables): dataset, model, `model_id`, `num_experts`, `prob_expert`/`unc_gating` (shown as the variant), `features`, `pred_len`, `seed`.

**Features type** (`--features`, column `feat` in the tables):

| feat | meaning | runs | configs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `M` | multivariate -> multivariate (all channels predicted) | 4880 | 2156 |
| `MS` | multivariate -> univariate (all channels in, target `OT` out) | 48 | 16 |
| `S` | univariate -> univariate (target `OT` only) | 48 | 16 |

The `S` / `MS` runs are a small side-sweep: ETTh1/ETTh2/ETTm1/ETTm2, iTransformer + PatchTST, `num_experts=3`, `pred_len=96`, seed 4021 only. Their widths/MSE are on a different target scale (one channel instead of all), so do not compare them against the `M` rows.

**Fixed across all runs collected here:**

| field | value | | field | value |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `seq_len` | 96 | | `d_conv` | 4 |
| `label_len` | 48 | | `factor` | 1 |
| `d_model` | 512 | | `embed` | timeF |
| `n_heads` | 8 | | `distil` | True |
| `e_layers` | 2 | | `des` | test |
| `d_layers` | 1 | | `itr` | 0 |
| `d_ff` | 2048 | | `alpha` | 0.1 (target coverage 0.90) |
| `expand` | 2 | |  | |

**Variant** = the `prob_expert` / `unc_gating` pair:

| variant | flags | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| MOE | `pe0 ug0` | deterministic mixture of experts, point forecast only |
| MOG | `pe1 ug0` | probabilistic experts (`--prob_expert`), mixture-of-Gaussians head |
| MOGU | `pe1 ug1` | probabilistic experts + uncertainty-derived gating (`--unc_gating`) |

`MOGU` with `num_experts=1` does not exist: [run.py](../run.py) exits with `SKIPPING: Redundant experiment (Single Expert + Unc Gating)`, so those cells are structurally absent, not missing runs.

`model_id` is `test` for the standard training runs and `cqr` for the models retrained with pinball loss (`--use_quantile_loss`) that the CQR methods need — a `cqr` row is a *different trained model*, not a different calibrator on the same model.

## 3. Methods

| method | configs run | window | result file | definition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Standard CP** (`standard_cp`) | 1738 | 1000 (1662) / unknown (76) | `result_calibration_mse_cp.txt` | `AdaptiveCP`, sliding-window split CP on absolute residuals, alpha=0.1, delayed update. Works for any model. |
| **ACI CP (g=0.001)** (`aci_cp`) | 111 | 1000 (110) / unknown (1) | `result_calibration_aci_cp_tsf.txt` | `ACICPCalibrator`, standard_cp's absolute-residual score with the target level driven by the Gibbs & Candes (2021) recursion per (horizon, channel) cell. gamma=0.001 (the 0.1/pred_len rule of thumb at pred_len=96). gamma=0 reproduces standard_cp exactly. Works for any model. |
| **Adaptive CPVS** (`cpvs`) | 1130 | 1000 (1075) / unknown (55) | `result_calibration_cpvs.txt` | `AdaptiveCPVS`, residual normalised by the predicted MoG variance, alpha=0.1, delayed update. Needs `--prob_expert` (MOG/MOGU only). |
| **ACI CP-VS (g=0.001)** (`aci_cpvs`) | 78 | 1000 | `result_calibration_aci_cpvs_tsf.txt` | `ACICPVSCalibrator`, cpvs's sigma-normalised residual score with the ACI level recursion, width = q*sigma. gamma=0 reproduces cpvs exactly. Needs `--prob_expert` (MOG/MOGU only). |
| **Aleatoric only** (`aleatoric_only`) | 473 | 1000 (338) / unknown (135) | `result_calibration_aleatoric_only.txt` | `AleatoricOnlyCalibrator`, alpha=0.1, epistemic term dropped. MOG/MOGU only. |
| **ACI aleatoric only (g=0.001)** (`aci_aleatoric_only`) | 78 | 1000 | `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_only_tsf.txt` | `ACIAleatoricOnlyCalibrator`, aleatoric_only's r^2/var_ale score with the ACI level recursion. gamma=0 reproduces aleatoric_only exactly. MOG/MOGU only. |
| **Aleatoric scale CP** (`cp_aleatoric_scale`) | 284 | 1000 (139) / unknown (145) | `result_calibration_aleatoric_scale_tsf.txt` | `AleatoricScaleCalibrator`, alpha=0.1, delayed update. Scales the interval by the aleatoric component only. MOG/MOGU only. |
| **ACI aleatoric scale (g=0.01)** (`aci_aleatoric_scale`) | 66 | 1000 | `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_scale_tsf.txt` | `ACIAleatoricScaleCalibrator`, alpha=0.1, gamma=0.01, delayed update. Aleatoric scale score/interval with the target level driven by the ACI recursion alpha_t += gamma*(alpha - err_t), per (horizon, channel) cell. alpha_t is clipped to [0, 1] (and the quantile level capped at 1.0), so width stays finite on every cell at the cost of some windup / undercoverage relative to the exact (unbounded) recursion -- see the calibrator docstring for the trade. gamma=0 recovers `AleatoricScaleCalibrator`. MOG/MOGU only. |
| **ACI aleatoric scale (g=0.001)** (`aci_aleatoric_scale_g001`) | 114 | 1000 (113) / unknown (1) | `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_scale_g001_tsf.txt` | Same `ACIAleatoricScaleCalibrator`, gamma fixed at 0.001 instead of 0.01. Under the delayed-update protocol the effective step is ~gamma*pred_len, so the smaller gamma keeps alpha_t off the clip more often at the cost of tracking drift more slowly. Run alongside the g=0.01 variant for direct comparison, not as its replacement. MOG/MOGU only. |
| **CQR quantile** (`cqr_quantile`) | 259 | unknown | `result_calibration_cqr_quantile.txt` | CQR on a model trained with pinball loss (`--use_quantile_loss`, `model_id=cqr`), alpha=0.1, delayed update. |
| **ACI CQR quantile (g=0.001)** (`aci_cqr_quantile`) | 62 | 1000 (61) / unknown (1) | `result_calibration_aci_cqr_quantile_tsf.txt` | `ACICQRCalibrator`, cqr_quantile's signed conformity score with the ACI level recursion. gamma=0 reproduces cqr_quantile exactly. Needs `--use_quantile_loss`. |
| **CQR retrain** (`cqr_retrain`) | 190 | 1000 | `result_calibration_cqr_retrain.txt` | Retrained CQR, rolling window: mode=finetune, window=1000, retrain interval=96 steps, 3 epochs per retrain, alpha=0.1. |
| **ACI CQR retrain (g=0.001)** (`aci_cqr_retrain`) | 60 | 1000 | `result_calibration_aci_cqr_retrain_tsf.txt` | `ACICQRCalibrator` behind the rolling-retrain driver: Gibbs & Candes Sec 2.2 (refit on a trailing window) plus their Sec 2.1 (adapt the level), where cqr_retrain runs only the former. gamma=0 reproduces cqr_retrain exactly. |
| **MoECP** (`moecp`) | 28 | 1000 | `result_calibration_moecp_tsf.txt` | `MoECPCalibrator`, gate-localised CP, alpha=0.1, temperature=1.0. Lowered from the paper's tabular default of 100 -- at 100 the test point's own normalized weight exceeded alpha (unbounded interval) up to 25% of the time on this grid; tau=1 keeps that near 0 while width stays well under standard_cp's, so it is not a collapse to uniform weights. Validity (Theorem 1) holds at any tau, so this is a comparability choice, not a correctness fix. |
| **ACI MoECP (g=0.001)** (`aci_moecp`) | 30 | 1000 | `result_calibration_aci_moecp_tsf.txt` | `ACIMoECPCalibrator`, moecp's gate-localized weighted quantile read off at a per-(horizon, channel) level 1-alpha_t. The one family member where the [0,1] clip costs nothing: alpha_t -> 0 makes the weighted CDF fall short, the interval goes unbounded, err_t = 0, and alpha_t is pulled back up. tau=1 only, serial only. gamma=0 reproduces moecp bit-for-bit. |
| **Adaptive window CP** (`adaptive_window_cp`) | 275 | n/a | `result_calibration_adaptive_window_tsf.txt` | `AdaptiveWindowAleatoricScaleCalibrator`, alpha=0.1, delayed update. Same score and interval as `AleatoricScaleCalibrator`, but the sliding-window size is chosen per run by replaying the delayed protocol on the tail of validation and scoring each candidate W by the interval (Winkler) score. MOG/MOGU only. |

**Window** is the sliding-window length (number of most recent calibration scores kept). Every method above runs at `alpha=0.1`. The window is only reported where it is actually established; for **673 rows it is left blank** rather than guessed, and `window_size` is empty in the CSV with `window_source` = `unknown - run cannot be dated`.

It is treated as known when the run recorded it itself, or when the run can be dated to a period where the code held one value. The window moved 300 -> 500 -> 1000 over the repo (3f54bba 2026-01-01, 0ca1060 2026-01-09 / 3182ab8 2026-01-13, bef8056 2026-01-22), and a row is datable only if it appears in a log file whose mtime pins it after the last change. Rows that exist solely in a `result_calibration_*.txt` carry no timestamp, so they get no window.


| method | window | basis |
|---|---|---|
| Standard CP | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (1662 rows) |
| Standard CP | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (76 rows) |
| ACI CP (g=0.001) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (110 rows) |
| ACI CP (g=0.001) | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (1 rows) |
| Adaptive CPVS | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (1075 rows) |
| Adaptive CPVS | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (55 rows) |
| ACI CP-VS (g=0.001) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 |
| Aleatoric only | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (338 rows) |
| Aleatoric only | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (135 rows) |
| ACI aleatoric only (g=0.001) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 |
| Aleatoric scale CP | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (145 rows) |
| Aleatoric scale CP | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (139 rows) |
| ACI aleatoric scale (g=0.01) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 |
| ACI aleatoric scale (g=0.001) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 (113 rows) |
| ACI aleatoric scale (g=0.001) | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (1 rows) |
| CQR quantile | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated |

| ACI CQR quantile (g=0.001) | **not recorded** | unknown - run cannot be dated (1 rows) |
| CQR retrain | 1000 | recorded in the run output |
| ACI CQR retrain (g=0.001) | 1000 | recorded in the run output |
| MoECP | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 |
| ACI MoECP (g=0.001) | 1000 | code, run dated after 2026-01-22 |
| Adaptive window CP | n/a | calibrator has no sliding window |

The one method whose window is exact for every row is **CQR retrain**, because it writes `window=` into its own result line. Making the other calibrators do the same would remove this whole class of uncertainty going forward.

Metrics: **cov** = empirical marginal coverage of the 90% interval on the test split (target 0.90), **width** = mean interval width in the standardised scale, **q** = mean conformal quantile. MSE/MAE are the point-forecast metrics of the same trained model, from `result_long_term_forecast.txt` (or the test block of the log).

## 6. ETT expert sweep — MOG, `num_experts` 1..5

The one place in the repo where expert count is swept on a fixed everything-else: ETT only, MOG variant (`prob_expert=1`, `unc_gating=0`), iTransformer backbone, `features=M`, `model_id=test`, `seq_len=96`, alpha 0.1 (target coverage 0.90). Everything else in this file mixes expert counts across backbones and variants; these tables do not.

### 6.1 — pred_len 96

## 8. What is *not* here

Every `result_calibration_*.txt` in the repo, and whether it holds any time-series rows. Counted fresh each run, so a file that starts producing `long_term_forecast` results moves out of the tabular-only group on its own:

| result file | long_term_forecast rows | tabular_regression rows |
|---|---|---|
| `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_only_tsf.txt` | 80 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_scale_g001_tsf.txt` | 122 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_aleatoric_scale_tsf.txt` | 66 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_cp_tsf.txt` | 113 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_cpvs_tsf.txt` | 81 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_cqr_quantile_tsf.txt` | 64 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_cqr_retrain_tsf.txt` | 62 | – |
| `result_calibration_aci_moecp_tsf.txt` | 36 | – |
| `result_calibration_adaptive_variance.txt` | – | 24 |
| `result_calibration_adaptive_variance_tsf.txt` | 276 | – |
| `result_calibration_adaptive_window_tsf.txt` | 277 | – |
| `result_calibration_aleatoric_mog.txt` | 156 | – |
| `result_calibration_aleatoric_mog_v2.txt` | 156 | – |
| `result_calibration_aleatoric_only.txt` | 280 | – |
| `result_calibration_aleatoric_scale_tsf.txt` | 287 | – |
| `result_calibration_cp_aleatoric_scale.txt` | – | 12 |
| `result_calibration_cp_dvs.txt` | 155 | – |
| `result_calibration_cpvs.txt` | 1018 | 12 |
| `result_calibration_cpvs_aleatoric.txt` | – | 12 |
| `result_calibration_cqr.txt` | – | 4 |
| `result_calibration_cqr_quantile.txt` | 284 | – |
| `result_calibration_cqr_retrain.txt` | 296 | – |
| `result_calibration_ease_hpd.txt` | – | 24 |
| `result_calibration_meld_hpd.txt` | – | 24 |
| `result_calibration_moce.txt` | – | 20 |
| `result_calibration_moecp.txt` | – | 20 |
| `result_calibration_moecp_nontau1.removed.txt` | 8 | – |
| `result_calibration_moecp_tau100.removed.txt` | 79 | – |
| `result_calibration_moecp_tsf.txt` | 29 | – |
| `result_calibration_mog_hpd.txt` | – | 12 |
| `result_calibration_mse_cp.txt` | 1559 | – |
| `result_calibration_seta_hpd.txt` | – | 24 |
| `result_calibration_sta_hpd.txt` | – | 24 |
| `result_calibration_standard_cp.txt` | – | 20 |

So 12 of these are still tabular-only — the HPD family (MoG/STA/SETA/MELD/EASE), MoCE, plain CQR, the static/aleatoric CP-VS variants and the tabular copies of Standard CP and MoECP have no long-term-forecast run recorded yet.

Structural gaps in the tables above:

* **CPVS / Aleatoric-* on MOE rows** — these calibrators consume the predicted MoG variance, which only exists with `--prob_expert`. MOE configs can only be calibrated with Standard CP.
* **MOGU + `num_experts=1`** — refused by `run.py` as degenerate.
* **CQR rows** — only exist where a `model_id=cqr` (pinball-loss) model was trained.
* **MoECP rows** — need `--prob_expert` (it consumes the gating distribution), and only the `tau=1.0` entries are kept.

## 9. Separate offline benchmark: `results_mog_calibration.csv`

[scripts/benchmark_mog_calibration.py](../scripts/benchmark_mog_calibration.py) runs its own split-conformal sweep from cached predictions and writes [results_mog_calibration.csv](../results_mog_calibration.csv) / [results_mog_calibration.md](../results_mog_calibration.md). It is **not** merged into the tables above because the protocol differs (fit on validation, evaluate on test, quantile taken per horizon-step and channel; CP-DVS additionally splits validation 50/50).

| field | values |
|---|---|
| datasets | ETTh1, ETTh2, ETTm1, ETTm2 |
| backbone | iTransformer |
| num_experts / variant | 3 / MOG (`unc_gating=0`) |
| pred_len | 96, 192, 336, 720 |
| seeds | 4021, 4022, 4023 |
| alpha | 0.05 and 0.1 (both) |
| methods | standard_cp, cpvs, cpvs_within, aleatoric_only, cp_dvs |
| rows | 480 (48 runs x 2 alphas x 5 methods) |
| extra metrics | interval score, pinball, width CV, SSC min-coverage, worst-horizon / worst-channel coverage, block coverage, violation clustering |

`cpvs_within` and `aleatoric_only` are algebraically the same estimator there, which is why their columns match.
